   - I's immediate neighbors: P, H, N, B

      - I to P to A to S to E to B
      - I to H to N to B
      - I to B
      - I to N to C to T to B
   - Any path from Q to I will pass through node B.

3. **Bottleneck Identification:**
   - Any feasible path from Q to I must pass through node B.

4. **Minimum Edge Removal:**
   - To disconnect Q and I, we must remove the edges from B to both Q and I. This means removing (Q, B) and (B, I).

Thus, the minimum number of edges to remove is 2, with the removal of the edges (Q, B) and (B, I).

**Answer:**

--- Model Response ---
To determine the minimum number of nodes that must be removed to completely disconnect the two black nodes \( N \) and \( Q \), let's follow the process of examining node connectivity step-by-step.

1. **Identify the two black nodes:**
   - The black nodes are \( N \) and \( Q \).

2. **Identify immediate neighboring nodes:**
   - For \( N \): The immediate neighboring nodes are \( S, E, F \).
   - For \( Q \): The immediate neighboring nodes are \( I \).

3. **Identify paths from \( N \) to \( Q \):**
   - \( N \) connects to \( S \), \( E \), and \( F \).
   - \( S \) connects to \( I \). This connection is notable as \( I \) is a neighbor of \( Q \).
   - \( E \) connects to \( B \), and \( B \) connects to \( P \) and \( C \).
   - \( C \) connects to \( I \). This connection is notable as \( I \) is a neighbor of \( Q \).
   - \( F \) connects to \( C \). This connection is notable as \( C \) connects to \( I \), a neighbor of \( Q \).
   - This can be summarized to the path mappings:
     - \( N \) -> \( S \) -> \( I \) -> \( Q \)
     - \( N \) -> \( E \) -> \( B \) -> \( C \) -> \( I \) -> \( Q \)
     - \( N \) -> \( F \) -> \( C \) -> \( I \) -> \( Q \)

4. **Identify Bottlenecks:**
   - All paths found between \( N \) and \( Q \) converge via the node \( I \).
   - To cut all paths, we need to remove node \( I \) and potentially other nodes converging to \( I \) (like \( S, C \)).

5. **Check Minimality of the Cut Set and Compile Answer:**
   - By removing \( I \), the majority of the paths between \( N \) and \( Q \) are blocked.
   - To ensure 100% disconnection, we must also additionally remove at least \( S or C \) since \( N \) -> \( S \) -> \( I \)  and \( N \) -> \( E \) -> \( B \) -> \( C \) -> \( I \).

6. **Final Answer:**
   - Removing \( I \) and either \( S \) or \( C \) is minimal and necessary.
   Nodes to remove: 2, Cut Set: [I, S] or [I, C]

   - **Q**: Connected to I, R, and O.

   - N is connected to B, and B connects to multiple nodes.
   - Q is connected to I, R, and O. Following the edges:
     - Q → I → H → G → B → N: This is one path.
     - Q → I → G → B → N: This is another path.
     - Q → O → C → B → N: This is another path.

   - Similarly, removing any single edge involving (I, H) or (O, C) does not suffice for the same reason.

5. **Minimal cuts**:
   - Removing the edges (Q, I) and (Q, O), fully breaks the connections into separate subgraphs.

   - (Q, O) and (Q, I).

   - Removing either B or I will disconnect the paths between A and H.

5. **Verify the Minimum Cut Set**:
   - Removing either B or I will disconnect A and H.
   - There is no smaller set of nodes that can achieve the disconnection.

6. **Conclusion**:
   - The minimum number of nodes to remove is 1.
   - The cut set can be either [B] or [I].

Hence, the answer is:
Nodes to remove: 1, Cut Set: [B] or [I]

To determine the minimum number of edges that must be removed to completely disconnect the two black nodes (C and F), let's analyze the paths between them:

1. **Identify the immediate neighboring nodes of C and F:**
   - C is connected to N and B.
   - F is connected to E, B, T, and G.

2. **Identify paths from C to F:**
   - Path 1: C → B → F
   - Path 2: C → B → E → F
   - Path 3: C → B → S → F
   - Path 4: C → N → O → H → G → F
   - Path 5: C → N → O → H → G → T → F

3. **Identify bottlenecks or critical edges:**
   - All paths from C to F must traverse through the node B or G.
   - Removing the edges (C, B) or (B, F) would block multiple paths.
   - Removing the edges (G, F) or (G, T) would also block multiple paths.
   - Since disconnecting one node requires either disconnecting all its connections or specific paths, we should look for the least number of disconnections.

4. **Evaluate the minimal set of edges to remove:**
   - Disconnect C completely: Remove (C, B) and (C, N).
   - Disconnect F completely: Remove (F, B), (F, E), (F, G), and (F, T). However, since focusing on minimal connects, we will look at specific paths.

5. **Select the minimal cut set:**
   - Removing (C, B) and one of the critical paths through F would suffice. Specifically, removing (C, B) and (G, F) is sufficient.

Edges to remove: 2, Cut Set: [(C, B), (G, F)]
